**Details for lanes G and M (2026-10-14/15; docs/17 §4 M10, M11):**
- **M11, MB per Sim:** spawn 20 Sims, diff `commitMB`, remove them; count `Sim` objects vs `SimDescription`s. Decides the idea "off-screen Sims keep skeleton/footprint/clips but drop meshes and textures, rebuilt with a fade-in on screen" [inferred feasible for the render half only; textures already in VRAM via DXVK, CAS part data shared; rebuild cost lands in live mode = stutter risk, so it needs an in-load A/B on hitches]. Small MB per Sim: drop the idea.
- **CAS / catalog baseline rule (Rodney, 2026-10-15):** every CAS, Buy/Build or catalog test and fix runs on top of the community fixes for that area, never on vanilla: `catalogue-lag-fix`, `cache-clear` (run before each cold run), `options-ini` (store off), `smoothpatch-package`, `s3pp`, `resource-cfg`, plus DXVK and S3SS (featured-items patch). All are default-on in `tools/setup/fixes.json` already; before a CAS run, check they are installed (installer `plan`) and record the set in the run notes. Our fixes count only for what is left over after them (docs/17 §5.4 rank 1). Optional A/B candidate on top: `nraas-mastercontroller` Compact View (default off, rank 2).
- **Parallel lane, seeded RNG for repeatable tests (Rodney, 2026-10-15; docs/06 §8, open question 5):** run noise (~1-2%) is as big as the offload acceptance rule (~2%), so seeding cuts reruns [inferred: ~10-20% less test time]. Research with a sub-agent while the main lane works (static only; no edits under `mods/` or `tools/setup` while the game runs, per the parallel-agents rule):
  1. Script RNG: `Sims3.SimIFace.RandomGen.gRandomGen` (private static, from `AppDomain.GetData("RandomGen")`) -> `ScriptCore.Random` -> native `Random_NextDoubleImpl 0x74A780` (app vtable `+0x17C`, then `+0x28`). Find where the native RNG object is seeded (time source?) in Ghidra.
  2. List every `new System.Random()` in `game-copy/decompiled/` (docs/06 counted >= 59; default seed `Environment.TickCount`) and which ones run during sim play.
  3. Design the switch: ts3fixes `seed_rng(seed)` agent action that sets `gRandomGen` to a seeded `IRandom` and patches the default `System.Random` seed (or a native hook on `0x74A780` / the seed source); off by default.
  4. Then with the game: same save, same seed, N=3 runs seeded vs unseeded; compare sim minutes, route counts, autonomy picks; record remaining variance in docs/TESTING.md. Bit-exact is not the goal (thread timing stays).
- **M10, zoom out and pan:** max zoom, pan the town, log `commitMB` and largest free block; confirms distant lots stay imposters and how much lot streaming adds.

- Performance Patch 2.1 measured (docs/17 §9): with the store set it frees ~70 MB address space (committed -71 MB, largest free block +64 MB), no load-time change, no errors; 7 of 9 patches armed on Steam (`[STBL509] active`). Blank-label check still open.
- F17 (docs/04): the sim loop's TaskObjects stage (0xD7FC60: Mono Task objects such as one-shot functions, immediate interactions, story progression, finalizers, load/save) stalled 15 times in one run, 0.27-6.9 s (median 624 ms; >5 s likely world load, at least one in play). **Our hitch profiler never timed this stage** (only ScriptTick 0xD80BC0), which likely explains F5's unaccounted share.
- Bridgeport Go Home (docs/12 §5): 22 of 30 Bridgeport homes are shell apartments; the stuck repro Sims split into shell-apartment residents (Wogan Hemlock, Wayne Bumble, Johnny Jones: `GoHomeHook` skips shell homes) and homeless/virtual townies (Suzy Strummer, Lilly-Bo Chique, Marie Desmarais, Paul Hogue). Fix design: watchdog second reset + fade-out for homeless, then unseen shell residents to the game's safe spot inside the home; repro `bp-apartment-gohome`.
- F18 (docs/04): first `/taskstall` run named every long stage: NRaas Overwatch's nightly StuckCheck (~300 ms, one route plan per Sim in one tick), our own `SafeSpot` check (362 ms, a full routability plan), one 1 s lot plan. Fixed: SafeSpot verdict cache (counters 37-39; load showed 597 hits vs 140 full checks); Overwatch StuckCheck spread to one plan per tick via a yielding hook on NRaas `SimEx.IsPointInLotSafelyRoutable` (switch `overwatchstuck`, counters 44-45; no cache there, NRaas reads many Sim inputs); optional `overwatch-no-stuckcheck` override (default off; Overwatch's check stays on, it catches cases our watchdog misses).
- Watchdog fix 1 (docs/12 §5.3): second reset when stuck somewhere new, fade-out for homeless townies, home-kind counters 33-36. `bp-waylons-gohome` could not run (Paul Hogue no longer exists: likely one of the faded homeless townies). Repro `bp-apartment-gohome`: PASS with the watchdog off (shell residents got home; the apartment case does not reproduce from Waylon's Haunt) and PASS with it on; in 5 min the watchdog acted 8 times world-wide (5 homeless faded, 2 shell + 1 house resets, 2 second actions), no stuck Sims (docs/12 §5.6). Fix 2 on hold.
- Installer: `plan` runs `content-health.ts`; `cache-clear` also empties SigsCache and DCBackup (keeps ccmerged.package). New agent actions for store items in use (`catalog_objects`, `place/remove_objects`, `cas_parts`, `wear_parts/restore_outfit`) and repro `store-items-memory`, untested.
- Route plan callers ranked (docs/04a §8; 20 managed profiles: plans = 15.2% of script time): DoRoute replans 16%, houseboat gangway re-plans 13% (IP only; House Boat Fix code, not touched), PlanToLotEx 11%, PlanRouteIntoLot 8%, 3 AM `RunStuckCheck` 5%, `IsPointInLotSafelyRoutable` 4%; autonomy scoring plans none. Built, default off, not run in game: ts3fixes `FewerPlans.cs` switches `routablecache` (game's routability checks share the SafeSpot verdict cache, counters 65-66) and `stuckcheckbusy` (3 AM stuck check skips Sims held by an object, counter 67). Next: `profile-world.ts --tag bp-fewer --ab routablecache,stuckcheckbusy --taskstall 250`, then IP.
- ts3native 0.4.0 adds an opt-in route search budget (docs/15 Phase 4): past `MaxUs` (100 ms) / `MaxNodes` every A* edge reads as a wall, so the search fails through its own exit; runtime switch `/native?routebudget=0|1`. Built and self-tested.
- ts3native 0.3.0 built and self-tested (docs/15 Phase 3): `[RouteWorker] Enabled/Mode` runs each route search on a worker thread while the sim thread waits; runtime switch `/native?routeworker=0|1`, `profile-world --ab routeworker`; opt-in `/routelock` probe (lock wait/hold, plan overlap). Risk: deadlock if the sim thread already holds the routing lock when planning (logged by a 2 s watchdog, not recovered).
- ts3native 0.5.0 async route plan built and self-tested, not run in game (docs/18 "Async plan", docs/15 Phase 5): native cannot return "pending" (all 278 `Plan()/Replan()` callers need the result at once), so the ts3fixes `Route.Plan` hook (`PlanAsync`) sleeps the Sim's own task tick by tick while the worker runs the whole `IC_Route_Plan`; handshake via `GetOption` on unused RouteOption bit 58; blocking `Lock_Acquire` waits on the sim thread run the job's portal-lock managed calls. Opt-in `[RouteAsync] Enabled=0`, runtime switch `/native?routeasync=0|1`, repro `bp-route-async`, counters ts3fixes [52-54]. Test only after the offload-sync run is clean. 0.5.1 (2026-10-13): first in-game run aborted on `interp.c:3595 pState->yieldReturn` (yield from inside a native->managed call); START now refused unless the current interpreter state is the task's root state, and ts3fixes `MayYield()` (NOYIELD query, bit 58|61) guards the Overwatch and watchdog yields (docs/18 "Crash: yield in a nested call"). Not yet rerun in game. 0.7.1 (2026-10-13, self-tested, not run in game): the async assert/hang came from the host delegate call `0xD75FA0` running on the worker and zeroing the sim thread's task id (host+0xBD0); it is now proxied whole to the sim thread (detour), orphan jobs are dropped on CANCEL or mode flip, the lock pump also serves finite deadlines, counters v8 add plans/fails per mode (docs/18 "Crash: async route assert, hang, orphan job").
- ts3native 0.6.0 save write built and self-tested, not run in game (docs/15 Phase 7, offload candidate #2): the native save `FUN_00AB1030` writes the working copy `CurrentGame.sims3`, then copies the old save to `<name>.sims3.backup` and the working copy to `<name>.sims3` (CopyFileW + FlushFileBuffers per file, ~33 MB each in Bridgeport). Opt-in `[SaveWrite] Enabled=0` times every step (one `save:` log line, counters v6) and with `Mode=1` copies the old save on a worker during the package write, then renames it to `.backup` (hash checks, else the game's own copy). Package write + script serialization stay on the sim thread. Next: test E with Mode 0 then 1 (docs/15 Phase 7 test plan); no save-time numbers exist yet.
- ts3native 0.7.0 faster RefPack decoder built and self-tested, not run in game (docs/15 Phase 6): the one call to the decode core (`0x4EC065` -> `0x4EB3B0`) goes to `RefpackFast` (wild copies, same results); offline on 3.7 GB of shipped resources identical, 2.2x a C port of the game decoder and 1.8x S3SS `RefPackDecompressor`. Decoding is only 0.24% of sim-thread time in live mode (15 profiles), so the expected gain is small (loads: ~0.2-0.5 s); opt-in `[Decompress] Enabled=0`, runtime switch `ts3native_counters.decompressMode` (counters v7), `Verify=1` for the first run, counters per thread in `ts3native.log`.
- ts3native 0.8.0 EA allocator cache built and self-tested, not run in game (docs/15 Phase 8, docs/20 item 2): `0x4E5E40` is a dlmalloc 2.7 derivative with one `CRITICAL_SECTION` per heap, fastbins only up to 64-byte chunks, an unsorted-bin sort on every non-fast Malloc and a full fastbin consolidation before every request >= 512 bytes [verified: decompile]. A per-thread, per-heap cache of freed 16-1024 byte chunks in front of Malloc `0x4E69D0`, Memalign `0x4E6A20` (align <= 16, offset 0) and Free `0x4E5920` skips the lock and the core; cached chunks stay "in use" to the heap (headers and user bytes untouched), heap Shutdown `0x4E5CB0` is detoured so chunks of a dead heap are dropped, OOM flushes and retries, thread exit flushes. Footprint: no reservation, <= 4 MB of committed heap held. Offline: ~16 ns per free+malloc hit vs ~55 ns for an uncontended lock alone. Expected 4-10% of sim-thread time if the hit rate is high [inferred]. Opt-in `[Alloc] Enabled=0`, runtime switch `ts3native_counters.allocMode` (+0x108, counters v9). Next: BP bundle run, then in-load A/B on `allocMode` with `/simstack`.
- ts3fixes lot object query cache built, not run in game (docs/20 §4a, offload candidate #3, item 9): `SimIFace.Queries.GetObjects(Type, lotId)` keeps one result per (type, lot) per sim tick (copy on hit; cleared on a new tick, after 100 ms, on any `Simulator.DestroyObject`; nothing cached while paused). Hot callers: tomb-room route callback, local autonomy (`GetObjects<Sim>` per commodity), parked vehicles, auto lights. Opt-in `kSpatialCache`, switch `spatialcache`, counters [62-64]. Next: `profile-world.ts --ab spatialcache`; check both patches say `ok` in `ts3fixes.applied`.
- ts3native 0.9.0 faster water route search built and tested offline, not run in game (docs/15 Phase 9, docs/20 item 1b): the water JPS (`0x620560`, 7.8% of IP sim-thread samples) spends ~92% in its jump `0x61FE10`, which recurses once per cell and makes ~10 helper calls (two `div`s, a vtable callback) per cell [verified: decompile + samples]. `WaterJump` is the same jump as one inlined loop; only the call at `0x6207FB` is retargeted. `waterjps-test.exe` runs the game's own code (copied from TS3W.exe to its addresses) against it: 43.9M jumps on 1000 random grids identical incl. callback calls; 3.9x faster. Expected ~50 ms/s of sim-thread time in IP, nothing in BP/SV [inferred]. Opt-in `[WaterJps] Enabled=0 Mode=1 Verify=0`, runtime switch `waterJpsMode` (+0x134, counters v10). Next: IP run with boats/swimming and `Verify=1` (mismatches 0), then in-load A/B on `waterJpsMode`.
- Tools: repro library (`repro.ts`, `repros.json`, trigger actions), `stuck-scan.ts`, repro saves, `run-check.mjs` gate, in-load A/B (`profile-world.ts --ab`), `save-stats.ts`; `offload-phase0.ts` variants nocc/cc/store (x CacheBudget), `-s3pp`, warm runs (a variant repeated right after itself) and the load split; probe `/net`; agent actions `cas_open/cas_category/cas_close/buy_open/buy_category/buy_close/cas_state` (store set + S3PP: CAS open 2.2 s, hair 3.2 s / 261 items, makeup 1.0 s / 17 items; clothing readiness fixed to polling and `cas_open {outfit}` added, not yet rerun in game; `cas_open` now falls back to an eligible household Sim; repro `store-cas-browse`; agent plugin hot-reloads with `build-agent.ts --hot` + `reload-agent.ts`).

**Working notes for the next context:**
- Parallel agents while the game runs: they must not edit `mods/` or `tools/setup/` while a `cycle.ts --restart` or installer step can run (the restart rebuilds the mods from source); native/ is rebuilt only by `install --only native-patches`. To install only ts3native without rebuilding the mods: detach the probe (`/detach`), `taskkill /F /IM TS3W.exe`, `install --only native-patches`, edit `Game\Bin	s3native.ini`, `ts3probe.py serve --launch`.
- Probe: `/reload` swaps agent.js but can leave the server with a destroyed script handle; prefer a full `serve` restart. Frida 17: use `Process.findModuleByName(x).findExportByName(y)` (no static `Module.findExportByName`).
- repro.ts reloads the save unless `--no-load`; use `--no-load` for a second pass in the same load.
- Restart + quick check: `bash tools/harness/restart-verify.sh <names> <save.sims3> [tries] ["--off a,b --max-stuck N --minutes M"]`; restart + in-load A/B: edit and run `tools/harness/restart-ab.sh`. One agent command: `node tools/harness/ag.mjs <cmd> '<json>'`. GC counters: `node tools/probe/gc-read.mjs`. Native counters: probe `/native` (needs a probe restart, agent.js gained `nativeCounters`).
- Route cache test (Next 3): close the game (the installed .asi is locked while it runs), `node tools/setup/install-fixes.ts install --only native-patches` (installs 0.2.0), set `[RouteCache] Enabled=1` in `Game/Bin/ts3native.ini` for the test only, restart, load Baseline-BP, `node tools/harness/repro.ts run bp-route-cache --no-load`, read `/native`.
- Editing PLAN.md with Python: read with open(p, encoding='utf-8') and write with the newline argument set to CRLF (backslash-r backslash-n); earlier scripts doubled the CR (CR CR LF), which editors show as blank lines.
- Every change to `mods/ts3fixes/src` reaches the next `cycle.ts --restart` build; keep it unchanged while a measurement that compares builds runs.

First FPS (test B, BP, current build): 56.6 / 59.0 FPS, p99 39.2 / 33.1 ms, p99.9 98.4 / 42.1 ms; 21 of 23 long frames overlapped a sim hitch in run 1. frame-times.js crashes the game at unload (fix in progress).

- **Async route plan PAUSED (2026-10-13):** three in-game crashes, each root-caused: (1) yield inside a nested invoke (interp.c:3595), fixed 0.6.1; (2) host callback 0xD75FA0 clearing the global task id on the worker, fixed 0.7.1 (whole host call proxied to the sim thread); (3) heap corruption: the goal step of every plan (Route_PlanCommon 0x7606E0 -> 0x75C2D0 -> 0x63D2F0 -> AStarSearch reset 0x61E320) runs OUTSIDE the routing lock on a per-lot search object (0x62EAB0) shared by all callers, so the worker and the sim thread ran it at once (xcpt 09.27.40: free in 0x61C020 node table). More unlocked lot state exists (0x6255B0, 0x62E420/0x63D7B0, inferred): native async needs a "routing gate" (owner waits at every routing entry), which leaves overlap only with non-routing work. Kept opt-in/off; worker mode 1 (sync) is stable (11,523 plans, 46,152 host calls, 0 crashes) but no speed gain. Next for route stalls: make searches cheaper instead (water JPS precompute 0x620560/0x61FE10, route budget revisit, fewer replans).
- **In-load A/Bs, BP, 2026-10-13 (10 min, 2-min slices; PHT ~09:57-10:23):** alloc cache (ts3native 0.8.0 [Alloc]): on 30.7 vs off 25.7 it/s (+19%), hitches 4.39 vs 6.12/s (-28%), 40% hits; one 2 s TaskObjects stall and route fail 2.5 vs 1.1% in the on slices: repeat with --taskstall before default on. spatialcache (alloc on both): 34.8 vs 33.0 it/s (+5%), hitches 3.06 vs 3.28 (-7%), 24% hits. routablecache+stuckcheckbusy: no measurable change (38.8 vs 38.2 it/s, 2.68 vs 2.64; no 3 AM check in the window). **How we test:**
- Each fix gets a repro: a trigger action in the agent (`place_sim`, `sim_go_home`, `sim_visit_lot`, `sim_route_to`) plus an entry in `tools/harness/repros.json`; run with `repro.ts run <id>`, several at once with `run-many` (areas 150 m apart, no shared Sims).
- `stuck-scan.ts` runs alongside and turns other stuck Sims into repro candidates (`probe-out/repro-candidates.json`).
- Runs that end with stuck Sims save `Repro-<tag>-<time>` for direct retests.
- Full per-world profiles (`profile-world.ts`, `profile-compare.mjs`, gate `run-check.mjs`) only at milestones; in-load A/B with `profile-world.ts --ab a,b --slice 2`; quick checks with `verify-shortcut.ts`.
- Save growth: `save-stats.ts <save> <later save>`.

**Rules from Rodney:**
- Repro-driven fixes; full runs only at milestones; bundle fixes per world; compare in one load with switches.
- Success = stuck Sims no worse than vanilla and at most one caused by a shortcut per 10-min run; don't chase every vanilla stuck Sim.
- Stuck Sims are solved (2026-10-13, many runs with 0 real stuck Sims): log possible cases in `docs/data/stuck-log.md` (how they occurred), no research or fixes unless one repeats often or a fix of ours caused it. Focus: stutter, FPS, performance.
- Stop a run on an obvious error or outdated code and fix first; watch every long run; update this plan with each fix.
- Teleports must not break what the Sim does next or strand it; each shortcut type must be seen working once.
- Parallel sub-agents for code and research; game tests stay serial; test-only helpers stay in the agent.
- Triage every crash against the change ledger; never enter credentials; never commit EA files, decompiled code, saves or captures (the repo is published on GitHub).

## Tools (all in the repo, Windows)

| Tool | What |
|---|---|
| `tools/probe/ts3probe.py serve [--launch]` | Frida probe on 127.0.0.1:47891: `status`, `memory`, `simstack` (sim thread native stack), **`managedstack`** (C# stack via interpreter frames), `interp`, **`rescache`** (resource caches), `read`, `string`, `agent?cmd=…`, `agentstatus` (incl. `worldLoaded`), `reload`, `detach` (always stop this way). After changing `ts3probe.py`, detach and re-run `serve` (attaches without relaunch). |
| `tools/probe/managed-sample.mjs [secs] [hz] [out]` | C# sampler: ranks self, task and inclusive methods. |
| `tools/frida/stutter-profile.js` | Native per-iteration profiler (routes, GC, finalizers, hitches): `python -m frida_tools.repl -n TS3W.exe -l tools/frida/stutter-profile.js -P '{"out":"<path>","hitchMs":50}' -q -t 600`. |
| `mods/ts3agent` | In-game agent: loader (mailbox kept across loads) + hot-reloadable plugin. Harness commands incl. game_state, load_save, save_game, auto_dialog, **autopilot** (holds speed, clears popups within a frame in any in-world state; speed `none` leaves the speed alone), keep_alive (+ repairs broken home objects), decline_requests, quiet_ui, catalog_search/buy_object/place_object. Build `build-agent.ts`, hot reload `reload-agent.ts`. |
| `tools/harness/cycle.ts --restart` / `--save X` | Restart (rebuilds the agent; clears the script cache only when agent source or Mods changed); load a save (waits on the world-loaded flag), end in live mode, ledger snapshot. Launch ~55 s, menu→ready ~37 s. |
| `tools/harness/new-save.ts`, `age-save.ts`, `watchdog.ts`, `crash-ledger.ts`, `offload-phase0.ts` | New save; aging with autopilot/keep_alive/declines; 1-min watchdog (stuck Sims, frozen clock, low memory; triage on crash); crash ledger; offload phase 0 variants. |
| `tools/harness/apex-run.ts` (skill ts3-apex-run) | Apex Radiance in-game runner wrapper: our pair (`--release` / given / none), loads and arms, crash triage per load, Bin pair always restored (`--restore` after a kill); output `probe-out/apex/<tag>/`. |
| `tools/setup/install-fixes.ts`, `check-conflicts.ts`, `fixes.json` | Fixes installer and conflict check. |
| `tools/ghidra/*` (`run-headless.sh DecompileTo.java <out> 0 <addrs>`), `internal-calls-map.ts`, `extract-assemblies.ts`, `dbpf.ts` | RE tools. Decompiled C# in `game-copy/decompiled`, native decompiles in `game-copy/analysis/decomp`. |

## Next tasks (backlog, by area)
- **[Memory, v0.2.6 design] Game wrapper (Rodney 2026-11-03):** design doc first. (1) Auto-restart: a wrapper/host process owns a borderless-fullscreen window, the 32-bit game renders into a child window; on a low largest free block the game saves, quits, is relaunched and auto-loads the save (sidecar file, never in the save) while the wrapper shows a TS3-style "Saving / Loading" screen. Reason: in-process reload gives no address space back (Dalexlol B7, docs/204), a restart gives ~400-600 MB. Exclusive fullscreen not supported. Candidate for Dalexlol (B7b). (2) Later stage: the same wrapper as a 64-bit render process (D3D9 proxy streams commands via shared memory; DXVK/driver/textures, ~430-640 MB, and better lighting move to 64-bit); rendering is Apex's area: measure first (address space by owner, sync D3D9 calls per frame), build off by default, propose to Apex.

## Steps (history and status)

### 1. Compare existing fixes: done
`docs/01-existing-fixes-comparison.md`, prior-art RE `docs/03-prior-art-re.md`.

### 2. Baselines
- [x] Fresh reinstall; vanilla and DXVK layers measured (docs/05). Vanilla cannot reliably load a save (address space).
- [x] Aged test saves: `Baseline-SV` (Sunset Valley, Keatons), `Baseline-BP` (Bridgeport, Angelistas), 14 sim days each under DXVK with keep_alive.
- [ ] Tests B–E on both saves (see next tasks).

### 2b. Test harness: working
Probe, agent (hot reload), cycle, new-save, age-save, watchdog, dialog rules, keep_alive, declines, quiet UI. Prior art: `docs/06-test-harness-prior-art.md`.
- [ ] Frame-time capture: hook `0xEC9F00` verified (docs/04 F19, CModule, present=1 only, dummy-tested; the end-of-B game crash was two frida-agents unloading in load order, fixed by detaching the profiler first); first in-game run `node tools/harness/tests-be.ts --tag sv-<layer> --only B`.
- [ ] A permanent probe ASI later (Frida stays for exploration).

### 2c. All-known-fixes layer: installed, first validation passed
Catalog `docs/07-known-fixes-catalog.md`, manifest `tools/setup/fixes.json`, installer and conflict checker.
- [x] Install with backups; conflict check clean; Bridgeport 2-day run without crash.
- [ ] Sunset Valley run, tests B–E, the open questions in next-task 2.

### 3. Reverse engineering: first pass done; interpreter and managed sampler added (F6, F7)
`docs/04-re-findings.md` (route planning synchronous under a global lock; GC every frame with adaptive ~1 ms budget; finalizer drain; catch-up steps; Boehm env vars), `docs/04a-script-layer-stutter.md` (NPC retry loops, weak route-fail blocking, unyielding local autonomy, weak EA stuck check). REA (MCP) is registered locally; restart the session to use its tools.
- [ ] Re-run Ghidra analysis with more heap (two analyzers hit OutOfMemory at 16 GB; some functions missing, e.g. `0xD81840` had to be created by hand).

### 4. Our own fixes
Re-ranked by measurement (docs/04 F5–F7): walk-style rules, NPC go-home, reaction broadcasters, then route search; a route-failure cache alone targets only ~2% of plans. All need the patch host (step 6). One change at a time, measured.

### 5. 64-bit resource offload helper: measured, cache helper dropped (phase 0, docs/08)
`docs/08-resource-offload-design.md`. Ideas only if it works: multi-core prefetch, whole-world preload into the helper, raising the game's cache, `tools/harness/warm-cache.ts` (written, unused), GPU options (textures already in VRAM via DXVK; DirectStorage at most a helper reader; no CUDA RefPack; no GPUDirect on GeForce). Note: `Sims3.ini [ResourceSystem] MemoryUsageLimit` is the async loader's limit, not the cache; `HoldOntoResource` is misspelled there (ignored).

### 6. Single patch host: one tool instead of clashing mods
Core mods clash because each replaces a whole EA assembly. Our agent gets a runtime method-patch host (MonoPatcher approach on the hot-reloadable plugin); each fix is a small switchable patch.
- [x] Pre-flight conflict checker. Old Mods folder had: ErrorTrap twice (sync copy), two ld_RandomSimFixes variants, overlapping Store Updates/Fixes packages (third-party repack, excluded), outdated simler90 UPDATE197.
- [ ] Study MonoPatcher's method patching under Mono 1.2; build our patch host.
- [ ] Reimplement only where mods truly fight or where our tool needs the behaviour (stuck Sims, routing, save cleanup).
- Licensing: NRaas, simler90, MonoPatcher and S3SS have no license file (all rights reserved). Read their decompiled code to understand the behaviour, write a behaviour spec in our own words (`docs/specs/<fix>.md`), implement from the spec, credit the author. Never paste or port their code line by line, in any language. Ask authors for permission where possible. simler90 publishes its source on its MTS page (check its terms).

### 7. Later
- 64-bit: not realistic as a rewrite (40,263 functions + Mono 1.2); grows only from per-function rewrites verified live with the probe.
- Rodney's CC (backup): 172 packages, 209,735 resources, 5.18 GB (15.4 GB decompressed), 98% in three "Store Updates" repack packages; textures 86% (handled by DXVK).

## Archive: plan notes as of 2026-10-12 noon

1. ~~Profile other worlds~~ **done 2026-10-12** (docs/04 F7b): Isla Paradiso spends 246 ms/s in route search (2.6× Bridgeport, 12% of plans fail), go-home 11%; Sunset Valley has slow, failing plans; Bridgeport's extra cost is crowd script work. `tools/harness/profile-world.ts` + `tools/probe/profile-compare.mjs` repeat it for any world. Optional: underwater/scuba 5 min vs land (Rodney's lag report).
2. ~~Patch host (step 6)~~ **works 2026-10-12** (docs/04 F8): startup-only method swaps with an instance stub to call the original; verified on `GoHome.Run` in Isla Paradiso. Hooks live in the test agent for now; before shipping, move the patch host and fixes into a separate player mod (the agent stays test-only). **Teleport rule (Rodney):** a shortcut must not break what the Sim does next. Shortcuts replace only the route: place the Sim at the route's destination and return success from the route call, so the interaction continues exactly as after a walk (enter the building, socialize, next queued action). Each shortcut gets a check that the Sim's interaction queue and the following action still run. **No stuck Sims (Rodney):** every shortcut places a Sim only on a spot that passes the game's `Sim.IsPointInLotSafelyRoutable` (a short plan from the spot to the lot's edges, so the Sim can walk on later), and every measured run counts stuck Sims (`profile-world.ts` writes `<tag>-stuck.json`, marking Sims recently teleported); go-home was proven with a full profile (docs/04 F9: 0 stuck Sims). **Further shortcut types (Rodney):** verify each with `tools/harness/verify-shortcut.ts --name <type>` (x3, at most ~5 min, enough tries, no stuck teleported Sim), no A/B; if it does not fire enough on one save, try other worlds or lots until it has been seen working. **Status 2026-10-12 (docs/04 F9, F10):** go-home, trips into buildings, community/home visits, go-to-lot and docked-houseboat shortcuts verified (0 stuck Sims); traffic-car shortcut dropped (stranded Sims in Bridgeport). Open: expansion travel (Late Night elevators/subway, Into the Future tubes, University bikes, Pets horses), and Bridgeport's pre-existing stuck Go Home townies. Rodney approved building all of fixes b, b2, b3 (go-home, houseboats, trips to buildings, lot visits, cars, expansion travel), one at a time and measured.
3. **Fixes, one at a time, each measured** (managed sampler + stutter profiler, same save, same duration): (a) ~~walk-style rules / `Enum.Equals`~~ **done** (docs/04 F11: walk-style 6.0% -> 1.7%, `Enum.Equals` 5.1% -> 1.0% in Bridgeport), (b) **off-screen go-home** (Rodney's idea, 2026-10-12): a prefix on `GoHome.Run` (`Sims3.Gameplay.Core.GoHome`, up to 3 route plans: into the lot, fallback, then water planning) that, for a non-selectable Sim far from the camera and from every active-household Sim and not on a lot with one, skips routing and does what `GoToVirtualHomeInternal` already does on failure: `FadeOut(destroyObject: true)` (de-instantiates; the game re-spawns townies through its normal situations). Townies near the camera still walk, so streets stay alive; walk-by population is a separate system and unchanged. Guards: never the active household, Sims in a situation/party/carpool, Sims mid-interaction with someone visible. Measure GoHome % and route fails before/after; fall back to a per-Sim retry backoff if fades look wrong. No existing mod does this (Overwatch only resets stuck Sims home), (b2) **houseboats** (Rodney, 2026-10-12): a houseboat is a boat object (`Objects.Island.Houseboat`) with its lot parented to it (`World.ParentLotToHouseboat`); it moves only through the helm's Cruise Here / Move to Port interactions, which route the whole boat over water. **Measured: NPC households do cruise** (3 of 6 occupied houseboats moved in a 10 min Isla Paradiso profile, docs/04 F7b; the `houseboats` agent command snapshots positions). Only if NPC boats move: for ones far from the camera, place the boat at its destination instead of driving it (still pays the lot/route rebuild at the destination, skips the water route and per-frame movement), (b3) **off-screen trip shortcut, general form of b** (Rodney, 2026-10-12): share of samples planning routes, by trip (docs/04 F7b data): Isla Paradiso 30% (go-home 8.0, rabbit-hole trips 6.4, lot visits 7.3, group walks/rides 2.4, stand-and-wait replans 1.5), Bridgeport 15% (go-home + virtual home 4.6, group walks/rides 1.5, lot visits 1.2, `LiteDriver` cars 1.0), Sunset Valley 13% (go-home 3.7, lot visits 4.2, rabbit holes 1.4). Rule: skip the middle of a trip, never its ends. Origin and destination both unseen (far from the camera and every active-household Sim, not on a lot with one): place the Sim at the destination (rabbit hole: straight inside; lot: at its edge). Destination seen: place at an unseen point near it and route only the last leg. Origin seen: route normally until out of view. Candidates in order of measured share: go-home, rabbit-hole trips (work/school/shops; the Sim vanishes into the building anyway), lot visits, cars (`LiteDriver`, `GoHereWith*` car/group rides: arrive instead of driving), houseboats (b2). Expansions to check with profile-world.ts per world: Late Night elevators/subway (community: off-screen Sims stuck rerouting), Into the Future travel tubes/hover vehicles (Oasis Landing), University bikes, Pets horses, Island Paradise water taxis and swimming. Never: the active household or anyone near the camera, Sims in a group with a seen Sim, service NPCs arriving at the active lot, walk-bys/joggers (their walk is the content). Build one candidate at a time, each only if its share is over ~2% in some world, measured before/after, (c) ~~reaction broadcaster throttle~~ **done** (docs/04 F12: `Lot.UpdateReactions` 8.6% -> 5.5% in Bridgeport), (d) then route search off the sim thread or fewer plans.
4. Later: GC tuning (Boehm env vars: initial heap, free-space divisor) to cut forced collections; tests B–E; long-running saves (task 8); store-content test (task 6).

| | Bridgeport | Sunset Valley | Isla Paradiso |
|---|---|---|---|
| sim iterations/s | 31 -> **50** (+61%) | 63 -> **117** (+85%) | 32 -> **74** (+133%) |
| hitches/s (>=50 ms) | 4.0 -> **2.5** (-39%) | 1.9 -> **1.0** (-47%) | 3.5 -> **1.7** (-51%) |

Route plans per real second rise only because the sim runs faster (same plans per iteration, cheaper per plan).

**Fixes in place: the player mod `mods/ts3fixes`** (built by `tools/setup/build-fixes.ts` into `build/ts3fixes.package`, installed by `cycle.ts --restart`; one `[Tunable]` on/off setting per fix in `Ts3Fixes.Instantiator`; the assembly needs `[assembly: Tunable]` or the game never runs it). The test agent no longer patches; its `patches` command reads the mod's counters and switches fixes per session (e.g. `patches {gohome:false}`). Verified in game 2026-10-12: the mod applies all 11 patches at startup on its own. Isla Paradiso check with the mod: in 3 min go-home 97, buildings 109, visits 72, home visits 8, go-to-community 8 shortcuts, route-fail walks skipped 4, 0 Sims stuck from shortcuts (1 game-side: a townie visiting the Prince family's houseboat).
- **Patch host** (docs/04 F8): copies a replacement's `MonoMethod` header over the game method's at startup (mid-game swaps miss callers the interpreter already compiled); an instance stub keeps the original callable.
- **Trip shortcuts** (docs/04 F9, F10): an unseen townie on an unseen trip starts at a safe spot at the destination, then the game's own method does the last metres and the arrival, so what the Sim does next is unchanged. Types: go-home, trips into buildings (work, school, shops), community and home visits, go-to-lot, docked houseboats. Spots must be on the destination lot and pass `Sim.IsPointInLotSafelyRoutable` (cached per lot); a 10 sim-minute guard stops loops. **Dropped:** traffic cars (stranded Sims in Bridgeport for ~1% gain).
- **Walk-style comparer** (F11): array instead of enum-keyed dictionary; `Enum.Equals` 5.1% -> 1.0% of script time.
- **Reaction broadcasters** (F12): off-screen ones pulse 1 in 4; `Lot.UpdateReactions` 8.6% -> 5.5%. Camera/household positions cached per sim tick; all hooks cost 0.9%.
- **New, verified 2026-10-12 (Bridgeport check PASS, 13 patches applied by the mod, incl. the first static hook):** station detour skip for unseen townies (`CheckAndUpdateRouteForStations`, static); watchdog now also clears unseen townies stuck walking anywhere for 120 sim-minutes (doorway deadlocks), at most one reset per Sim per sim-day (the first build reset the same Sims repeatedly); shortcut spots now spread out from the entrance instead of reusing one cached point (two Sims placed at the same Bridgeport building later deadlocked by its exit). Earlier list: homeless/virtual-apartment townies going "home" fade out at once when unseen (`GoToVirtualHomeInternal.Run`, docs/10, docs/12); unseen townies skip the obstacle re-search and route-fail walks (`SimRoutingComponent.SetRouteOptions`, `GetRouteFailPath`, docs/11); stuck-Sim watchdog (unseen townie stuck going home 60 sim-minutes -> `SetToResetAndSendHome()`); per-Sim route-failure backoff (`ScriptCore.Route.Plan`, same destination 30 sim-minutes, written by a sub-agent, `RouteBackoff.cs`). Pet guard added to the go-home shortcut.
- Houseboat finding: boats moving cost 0.3%; Sims boarding through gangways cost 5-8%, hence the docked-houseboat shortcut instead of moving boats.

**Next, in order:**
1. **Stuck Sims in SV and IP** (in progress): four 5 min checks (SV and IP, shortcuts on and off) with the new full moved-Sim record (`patches` -> `moved`). The IP ones are houseboat households (Arias, Scott), so the docked-houseboat shortcut is the first suspect. Fix or drop whatever strands Sims, then re-profile.
2. ~~Move the fixes into a player mod~~ **done** (`mods/ts3fixes`). Open: verify the four new fixes (virtual home and watchdog in Bridgeport, route-fail skip and backoff in Isla Paradiso), then a bundle profile per world; installer entry `stutter-fixes` in `fixes.json` (default on) and `mods/ts3fixes/README.md` **done**.

Pet guard added to the go-home shortcut.) Earlier plan text: Late Night elevators/subway, Into the Future tubes, University bikes, Pets horses; each verified with `verify-shortcut.ts`. If a type does not happen often enough, add a harness `force_trip` command (random unseen townies, staggered) rather than waiting.
4. **Bridgeport's pre-existing stuck Go Home townies** (same Sims in every run of `Baseline-BP`, with or without shortcuts). Analysis: `docs/12-bridgeport-stuck-gohome.md` (agent, code-only): likely not `GoHome` but `GoToVirtualHomeInternal` (homeless/virtual-apartment townies, same "Go Home" label; `Sim.MakeSimGoHome` uses `GoHome` only when `LotHome != null`, verified), stalled on blocked routes (stand-and-wait up to 50 sim-minutes) plus the retry loop. Confirm live first (interaction type and route action of a stuck Sim), then: shortcut on `GoToVirtualHomeInternal.Run` ending the trip the game's own way (fade out), a watchdog using `SetToResetAndSendHome()` for unseen Sims stuck 60+ sim-minutes, and a backoff in `GoHomeHook`.
5. **(d) Route search** (agreed with Rodney 2026-10-12; route search is native C++ A* `0x81b2a0` under lock `0x1021cf0` on the sim thread, still 10-13% of script time after the shortcuts; failed searches cost the most because they exhaust the search). In order of gain per effort:
   1. Fewer plans: the trip shortcuts (done).
   2. **Cheaper failures (next routing fix;** native analysis `docs/11-route-planner-re.md`, agent: the planner has no node, time or cost budget, so a failure expands everything reachable [disassembly]; one failure becomes many searches: native retries per goal lot and nudged start, `ReplanToFindObstacleWhenPathPlanFails` (a second full search, set on Sim routes; option exists in `Route.RouteOption`, verified) and 4-6 more plans in C#. Order: (i) turn off the obstacle replan for unseen townies (C#), (ii) per-Sim backoff (C#), (iii) negative cache at the `Route_PlanLocked` entry `0x82c6e0` returning a plain failure before the lock (native), (iv) a budget cap last (native patch, must free open-list nodes on abort). Label fix: `0x81b2a0` is the RoutingJob Execute, the world A* is `0x676260`. Earlier text:** a cache of routes that just failed (same Sim or start area to the same destination: skip re-planning for a while), a per-Sim backoff after a failure, and a search budget cap for unseen townies' routes. Measure first how much route time is failures (stutter profiler `routeFails`, `byEntry`).
   3. Plan off the sim thread: searches on a spare core with the Sim waiting a frame; needs RE of the native route lock and data first.
   4. Two-level planning for long trips: precomputed lot-to-lot graph plus a short local search; only after 2 and 3, touches native data.
   - Not doing: our own replacement of the native planner (the routing world, i.e. footprints, slots, portals, water, cars, ladders, gangways, elevators, lives in native data; months of work and a high risk of stuck Sims), or a learned/AI route model (paths must be exactly valid; a model cannot guarantee that and would cost more than A* in this 32-bit game). Simple prediction ("this route failed recently") is the failure cache in 2.
   Also: **GC tuning** (analysis `docs/13-gc-tuning.md`, docs/04 F14: forced collections come from a tight heap that starts at 40 MB and grows 8 MB at a time). Steps: (1) ~~measure peak heap~~ done: Bridgeport full mod after ~25 min: heap 160 MB (started 40), 18 MB largest free, 0.33 collections/s (a cycle every ~3 s), budget pinned at the 1500 maximum (probe reads, `$TEMP`-style script: heap 0x12225A0, free 0x12225B4, gc_no 0x11F2F90, budget 0x11922A4), (2) launch with `GC_INITIAL_HEAP_SIZE` = peak + 128-256 MB (harness: `ts3probe.py serve --launch --env`), compare forced GCs per second, (3) growth step 8 -> 32 MB (native patch), (4) budget ceiling. Players: env var needs a launcher or a native patch of the default; decide after measuring; underwater lag check in Isla Paradiso (Rodney's report: CPU vs GPU with the stutter profiler while diving).
6. **Long-running saves (task 8)**: research done (`docs/09-save-bloat-research.md`, agent, 2026-10-12): keep Overwatch's default cleanups and turn on Cleanse The Dead for multi-generation saves; memories off or cleared; Regul Save Cleaner on a backup; Save As with rotating names. Own cleanup gaps, ranked: weak NPC-NPC relationships (`Relationship.sAllRelationships`, NPC decay at 0.001x), MiniSims from other worlds with no family links (`MiniSimDescription.sMiniSims`), memory snapshots per NPC, histories of dead/non-resident Sims. Measuring: `tools/harness/save-stats.ts <save> <later save> ...` (bytes per resource type, images sniffed). First result, Isla Paradiso checkpoints 21.9 -> 25.0 MB in 14 sim days: type `0x342779a7` +1.82 MB (9 -> 15 resources of ~250 KB), `0x06b981ed` +0.55 MB (one resource, likely the saved script state), PNG `0x0580a2ce` +0.47 MB (4 -> 34 images), `0x05ed1226` +0.24 MB; everything else flat. Identified: `0x06b981ed` starts with `OBJS` = the world's object persistence (7.4 MB uncompressed). `0x342779a7`: 1.7-2.3 MB binary blobs with sequential instances (0x10001, 0x20001, ...), about one new per 2 sim days, unknown. Next: identify `0x342779a7` (s3pe / community type lists), then counts via `reflect_get`.
7. Later: tests B-E, store-content test (task 6).

**Repro first (Rodney, 2026-10-12):** a run that ends with stuck Sims saves `Repro-<tag>-<HHMM>` (+ `probe-out/repro/<name>.json` listing who was stuck where); test a fix by loading that save, not by waiting for the problem to recur. Saves keep positions, not interactions (Sims reset on load), so place-bound problems reproduce. **Repro library** (Rodney: every planned fix gets one): `tools/harness/repros.json` + `repro.ts run <id>` (loads the save, runs agent steps such as `place_sim` / `sim_go_home`, watches stuck Sims or a counter, PASS/FAIL). Entries so far: bp-hogans-gohome, bp-waylons-gohome, ip-houseboat-gohome, bp-virtualhome-fade. Add an entry for each new fix and bug (underwater lag: place the Sim at a dive spot; GC: a heavy save as workload); a fixed bug's repro stays as a regression test. **Good enough (Rodney, 2026-10-12):** not every stuck Sim gets fixed. A 10-min run passes when stuck Sims are no more than the same world without the mod and at most one stuck Sim was moved by a shortcut; then move on to the next plan items and fix stuck Sims on the side as runs reveal them. Vanilla stuck Sims are fixed only when cheap. Open regression: full-mod Bridgeport run (12 of 14 stuck were moved by shortcuts; earlier builds 0). **World-wide scan during repros (Rodney):** `tools/harness/stuck-scan.ts` runs alongside repros, reports every other stuck Sim and drafts a repro entry for it (`probe-out/repro-candidates.json`); order: fix the current repro, then reproduce and fix the next candidate; several repro/fix loops in parallel where places are free. **Parallel repros (Rodney):** a big world can host several repros at once: `repro.ts run-many id,id,...` runs every entry that shares a save, is at least 150 m from the others (`area` in repros.json) and uses different Sims, in one load and one watch window; clashing ones go to the next batch. New trigger actions: `sim_visit_lot`, `sim_route_to` (agent rule: add a trigger action for every new bug). **How to test (tools):** `cycle.ts --restart` then `ts3probe.py serve --launch` then `cycle.ts --save X`; `profile-world.ts --tag t` (10 min profile incl. stuck Sims) and `profile-compare.mjs a b`; `verify-shortcut.ts --name a,b [--tries N] [--off x,y] [--max-stuck N]` (quick check, max ~5 min).

**In progress (2026-10-12, afternoon):** GC heap A/B in Bridgeport (default vs `GC_INITIAL_HEAP_SIZE` 352 MB; also rechecks the full-mod stuck regression; a 25-min scan of the full mod found no stuck Sims); save cleanup **written** (docs/14; code in scratch `savecleanup/`, wire into the mod after the GC tests): `0x342779a7` = the native CAS SimService cache of built Sims and outfits (not trimmable directly; shrinks with fewer Sims), `0x05ED1226` = REFS reference store, `0x06B981ED` = OBJS script state; opt-in cleanup (`kSaveCleanup`, off by default, daily at 2:00, never while saving or travelling) removing weak NPC-NPC pairs via `Relationship.RemoveRelationship` and unlinked other-world MiniSims via `MiniSimDescription.RemoveMSD` (both verified to exist); repro `bp-save-cleanup` on a save copy `Cleanup-Test-BP`; native patch host **built** (`native/ts3native`, `build.cmd`, `out/ts3native.asi` + `.ini`; docs/15): GC initial heap 40 -> 256 MB (0xE4E168), growth step 8 -> 32 MB (3 sites near 0xE4A1D8, all or none), optional budget ceiling; every site checks its original bytes first (verified against the exe copy); patches before GC_init (only reached from Mono init, after the ASI loader). Not yet loaded in the game; test after the GC A/B (env var test shows whether a bigger heap helps). Route negative cache is phase 2 (`Route_PlanLocked` 0x82C6E0 prologue `81 EC D8 00 00 00`, 6 bytes). Installer needs a `local-build` source kind. Next for the game: underwater lag repro (Isla Paradiso dive spot, active Sim scuba).

**Rules from Rodney:** develop and check each fix against its repro (no full runs per fix); the whole repro library (`repro.ts run-many`) is the regression check before a milestone; full per-world profiles only at milestones with many fixes bundled, against the baseline with the `run-check.mjs` gate, and every new problem they find becomes a repro; test fixes as one bundle per world; compare or isolate fixes with the runtime switches inside one load (`profile-world.ts --ab a,b --slice 2`), not one restart and 10-minute run per fix; stop a test on an obvious error and fix first; stop a run as soon as the code it measures is outdated and rerun on the new build; teleports must not break what the Sim does next and must not leave Sims stuck; each new shortcut type must be seen working (`verify-shortcut.ts`, other world if it does not fire), no A/B needed; harness-only helpers stay in the test agent; every crash is triaged against the change ledger; never enter credentials; never commit EA files, decompiled code, saves or captures (the repo is published on GitHub).

**Earlier findings** (details in docs): scripts run in the Mono 1.2 interpreter, no JIT (docs/04 F6); per-world profiles (F5, F7, F7b); offload phase 0 says the 64-bit cache helper is not worth building (docs/08); graphics VRAM fix (docs/05); references SV ~0.40, BP ~0.33 sim days per real minute.

**Multi-worker note (Rodney, 2026-10-19):** when P4/P5 gets 2-3 route workers, size each worker's private pool region at least 64 MB (96 MB if the budget allows) (currently one region of 96 MB; most used live 19.9 MB).
